\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\quad

(135)

Hence, any (transient) quadratic contribution in
F[\psi_{V}]
can only occur as a result of the effective photon generator
H
. Conversely, one finds that if that is the case, then the noisy QFI will be asymptotically linear.

Let
\rho_{V}=\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}\outerproduct{\lambda_i}{\lambda_i}
, and thus
\rho_{W}=\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}U_{\theta}^{\dagger}\outerproduct{\lambda_i}{\lambda_i}U_{\theta}\equiv\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}\outerproduct{\tilde{\lambda}_i}{\tilde{\lambda}_i}
. We have,

\dot{\rho}_{V}=\iota[H,\rho_{V}]+U_{\theta}\dot{\rho}_{W}U_{\theta}^{\dagger}

(136)

Now, the mixed state QFI expression reads,

F[\rho]=\sum_{ij}\frac{2}{\lambda_{i}+\lambda_{j}}|\langle\lambda_{i}|\dot{\rho}|\lambda_{j}\rangle|^{2}

(137)

F[\rho_{V}]=\underbrace{\sum_{ij}\frac{2(\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j})^{2}}{\lambda_{i}+\lambda_{j}}|\langle\lambda_{i}|H|\lambda_{j}\rangle|^{2}}_{F[\rho_{W},H]}+F[\rho_{W}]+2\sum_{ij}\frac{\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}{\lambda_{i}+\lambda_{j}}\text{Im}[\langle\lambda_{i}|H|\lambda_{j}\rangle\langle\tilde{\lambda}_{j}|\dot{\rho}_{W}|\tilde{\lambda}_{i}\rangle]

(138)

Now, we have that
F[\rho_{W},H]\leq 4\operatorname{var}[H]
and
F[\rho_{W}]\leq 4T\alpha_{W}
. Notably, all terms other than
F[\rho_{W},H]
are strictly linear in
T
, without any quadratic transients. Now, we use the following result to lower bound
F[\rho_{W},H]
.

Theorem 4

(Theorem 3 of
[
79
]
)
.

The QFI of the mixed state
\rho_{\theta}=U_{\theta}\rho U_{\theta}^{\dagger}
with
U_{\theta}=e^{\iota\theta H_{R}}
is equal to

F[\rho_{\theta}]=\min_{H_{\text{tot}}}\operatorname{var}_{\Psi}[H_{\text{tot}}]

(139)

where
\Psi\equiv\Psi_{SR}
is any purification of
\rho
with
\rho_{R}=\tr_{R}[\ket{\Psi}\bra{\Psi}_{SR}]
and the minimization is over all
H_{S}
such that
H_{\text{tot}}=H_{S}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{R}+\mathbbm{1}_{S}\otimes H_{R}
.

Recalling the classic result bounding operator covariances in terms of mutual information.

Theorem 5

(
[
80
]
)
.

For a bipartite state
\rho_{SR}
we have

\text{Cov}[O_{S},O_{R}]^{2}\leq\|O_{S}\|^{2}\|O_{R}\|^{2}I[S:R]

(140)

where
I[S:R]
is the mutual information.

Combining these two, we have the following Lemma.

Lemma 4
.

F[\rho_{W},H]\geq\operatorname{var}[H]-cf(T)
with
f(T)<T
for
T<\tau^{*}
and
\lim_{T\to\infty}f(T)=\tau^{*}
with
c=O((\log{D})^{k})
for some finite
k>0
depending on the parameter encoding.

Proof.

Consider
\ket{\psi_W}
to be the purification of
\rho_{W}
. Now consider
H_{\text{tot}}=H_{S}+H_{R}
. The variance of this object is (all (co-)variances are on
\psi_{W}
),

\operatorname{var}[H_{\text{tot}}]=\operatorname{var}[H_{S}]+\operatorname{var}[H_{R}]+2\text{cov}[H_{S},H_{R}]

(141)

Now, note that
\operatorname{var}[H_{R}]=\operatorname{var}_{\rho_{W}}[H_{R}]
is the QFI term we are after. Additionally,
\operatorname{var}[H_{S}]\geq 0
. Thus, the only term which can decrease the total variance is the covariance term. However, note that
H_{R}=\sum_{\tau}h^{R}_{\tau}
from Lemma
2
. Thus, we get,

\text{cov}[H_{S},H_{R}]=\sum\text{cov}[H_{S},h_{\tau}^{R}]\geq-\left|\sum\text{cov}[H_{S},h_{\tau}^{R}]\right|\geq-\sum\left|\text{cov}[H_{S},h_{\tau}^{R}]\right|\geq-\|H_{S}\|\|h\|\sum I[S:\tau]

(142)

where we used Theorem
5
in the last step. If the correlation length is
\tau^{*}
, we have,

\text{cov}[H_{S},H_{R}]\geq-\|H_{S}\|\|h\|\sum e^{-\tau/\tau^{*}}\geq-\|H_{S}\|\|h\|\frac{e^{-1/\tau^{*}}}{1-e^{-1/\tau^{*}}}\left(1-e^{-T/\tau^{*}}\right)

(143)

Thus, we have shown that for any purification, and any
H_{S}
one has

\operatorname{var}[H_{\text{tot}}]\gtrsim\operatorname{var}[H_{R}]-\|H_{S}\|\|h\|f(T)

(144)

with

f(T)=\frac{e^{-1/\tau^{*}}}{1-e^{-1/\tau^{*}}}\left(1-e^{-T/\tau^{*}}\right)

(145)

The operator norm
\|H_{S}\|
cannot scale arbitrarily with the same argument as of Lemma.
1
.
By Theorem
4
, we thus have that,

F[\rho_{W},H]\geq\operatorname{var}[H]-cf(T)

(146)

∎

Crucially, note that we did not need to invoke mixing of the dynamics to the steady state to prove this, and this is valid in the transient regime of
0<T<\tau^{*}
as well. That is, the transient quadratic part of the
\operatorname{var}[H]
can be obtained by measuring the photons alone if HKS is satisfied.
